<a id="L1"></a>**L0001** `00:00:33` From the time we were children, we all

<a id="L2"></a>**L0002** `00:00:35` asked the question, "What would happen

<a id="L3"></a>**L0003** `00:00:37` if knights, Vikings, samurai, all those

<a id="L4"></a>**L0004** `00:00:41` medieval warriors that we knew about

<a id="L5"></a>**L0005** `00:00:43` growing up ended up fighting each other

<a id="L6"></a>**L0006** `00:00:44` in a big battle royale? Who would win?"

<a id="L7"></a>**L0007** `00:00:48` During the 2015 E3 presentation of

<a id="L8"></a>**L0008** `00:00:51` Ubisoft,

<a id="L9"></a>**L0009** `00:00:53` For Honor was presented to the world for

<a id="L10"></a>**L0010** `00:00:55` the first time. A big CGI trailer that

<a id="L11"></a>**L0011** `00:00:58` showed medieval warriors fighting each

<a id="L12"></a>**L0012** `00:01:00` other in a context like we'd never seen

<a id="L13"></a>**L0013** `00:01:02` before. And those childish fantasies

<a id="L14"></a>**L0014** `00:01:05` emerged and bubbled up back to the

<a id="L15"></a>**L0015** `00:01:06` surface. The gaming sphere was

<a id="L16"></a>**L0016** `00:01:08` introduced to a new IP for a fighting

<a id="L17"></a>**L0017** `00:01:10` game with deep lore, incredible

<a id="L18"></a>**L0018** `00:01:13` interactivity, and the potential for

<a id="L19"></a>**L0019** `00:01:15` something incredible. But any good

<a id="L20"></a>**L0020** `00:01:17` fighting game like this needs a

<a id="L21"></a>**L0021** `00:01:18` backdrop, a context,

<a id="L22"></a>**L0022** `00:01:21` a lore. From what we understand, the

<a id="L23"></a>**L0023** `00:01:24` world of For Honor functioned similar to

<a id="L24"></a>**L0024** `00:01:26` ours up until around the year 1000 AD.

<a id="L25"></a>**L0025** `00:01:29` At this time, a great global catastrophe

<a id="L26"></a>**L0026** `00:01:32` hit the world that was known as the

<a id="L27"></a>**L0027** `00:01:34` cataclysm. Now, no one is quite sure

<a id="L28"></a>**L0028** `00:01:36` what caused it. Was it an act of nature,

<a id="L29"></a>**L0029** `00:01:39` of God, of fate? Whatever the cause, the

<a id="L30"></a>**L0030** `00:01:41` effects were all too wellknown. The

<a id="L31"></a>**L0031** `00:01:44` world was irrevocably altered. Moving

<a id="L32"></a>**L0032** `00:01:46` continents, seas, and land masses about.

<a id="L33"></a>**L0033** `00:01:49` Tsunamis swallowed whole islands and

<a id="L34"></a>**L0034** `00:01:52` peninsulas. Earthquakes ravaged the

<a id="L35"></a>**L0035** `00:01:54` land, ripping open the earth like hungry

<a id="L36"></a>**L0036** `00:01:56` jaws to devour cities, villages, and

<a id="L37"></a>**L0037** `00:01:58` nations alike. Where once were lush

<a id="L38"></a>**L0038** `00:02:01` fields and beautiful rivers and lakes

<a id="L39"></a>**L0039** `00:02:03` were now magma pits, desolate

<a id="L40"></a>**L0040** `00:02:05` wastelands, and begotten blighted ruins.

<a id="L41"></a>**L0041** `00:02:08` And this event struck the whole world.

<a id="L42"></a>**L0042** `00:02:11` Few survived the cataclysm, and those

<a id="L43"></a>**L0043** `00:02:14` that did were left with fewer resources

<a id="L44"></a>**L0044** `00:02:16` than they could manage. A fight for

<a id="L45"></a>**L0045** `00:02:18` survival began. In the continent known

<a id="L46"></a>**L0046** `00:02:20` as Heathmore, three factions in

<a id="L47"></a>**L0047** `00:02:23` particular gathered. The Vikings of the

<a id="L48"></a>**L0048** `00:02:25` Northern Realms, the Knights of the

<a id="L49"></a>**L0049** `00:02:27` West, and the Samurai, foreign warriors

<a id="L50"></a>**L0050** `00:02:29` from an island nation who traveled to

<a id="L51"></a>**L0051** `00:02:31` the eastern lands of Heathmore. These

<a id="L52"></a>**L0052** `00:02:34` three factions began a long and brutal

<a id="L53"></a>**L0053** `00:02:37` war for land, resources, and control.

<a id="L54"></a>**L0054** `00:02:41` Neither side would give up or relent.

<a id="L55"></a>**L0055** `00:02:43` And over 1,000 years of war would see

<a id="L56"></a>**L0056** `00:02:46` these nations advance, grow, and in the

<a id="L57"></a>**L0057** `00:02:49` end, forget even their reason for

<a id="L58"></a>**L0058** `00:02:51` fighting in the first place. For you

<a id="L59"></a>**L0059** `00:02:54` see, when all else is put aside, when

<a id="L60"></a>**L0060** `00:02:57` the world has been taken from them,

<a id="L61"></a>**L0061** `00:02:59` there's only one thing these warriors

<a id="L63"></a>**L0063** `00:03:03` Honor.

<a id="L553"></a>**L0553** `00:22:09` The ideology of Apolon was simple. Peace

<a id="L567"></a>**L0567** `00:22:47` died

<a id="L568"></a>**L0568** `00:22:49` in victory.

<a id="L569"></a>**L0569** `00:22:56` When the game introduces itself to you,

<a id="L570"></a>**L0570** `00:22:58` it sets itself apart from other games.

<a id="L571"></a>**L0571** `00:23:00` With game modes like Dominion, which is

<a id="L572"></a>**L0572** `00:23:02` like a team-based game mode, you really

<a id="L573"></a>**L0573** `00:23:04` have to rely on your team in team

<a id="L575"></a>**L0575** `00:23:08` elimination, a solo type of game, like a

<a id="L576"></a>**L0576** `00:23:10` lone warrior type of thing, you know,

<a id="L584"></a>**L0584** `00:23:28` to since today. I genuinely despise

<a id="L587"></a>**L0587** `00:23:33` definitely a split community between the

<a id="L589"></a>**L0589** `00:23:37` community also split between different

<a id="L592"></a>**L0592** `00:23:42` like a wildfire. There was a ton of

<a id="L593"></a>**L0593** `00:23:44` content like posted everywhere. You had

<a id="L596"></a>**L0596** `00:23:51` for honor and like the absolute geniuses

<a id="L597"></a>**L0597** `00:23:54` at r/forer rants. In these communities,

<a id="L601"></a>**L0601** `00:24:03` For Honor just became like a really fun

<a id="L621"></a>**L0621** `00:24:48` Okay, a heavy parry would get you a

<a id="L643"></a>**L0643** `00:25:37` One thing that fascinated many people

<a id="L649"></a>**L0649** `00:25:49` So, my main gripe with Therona at this

<a id="L668"></a>**L0668** `00:26:32` And who better to talk about Lawbringer

<a id="L670"></a>**L0670** `00:26:40` Back in season 1 when Lawbringer was the

<a id="L696"></a>**L0696** `00:27:39` think it was something like 14 days into

<a id="L701"></a>**L0701** `00:27:52` uploaded this guide just to help some

<a id="L702"></a>**L0702** `00:27:54` people, before long, it seemed like

<a id="L703"></a>**L0703** `00:27:56` people thought that I knew what I was

<a id="L704"></a>**L0704** `00:27:57` talking about, and a lot of people

<a id="L705"></a>**L0705** `00:27:59` decided to learn more about what I had

<a id="L706"></a>**L0706** `00:28:01` to offer for this game.

<a id="L707"></a>**L0707** `00:28:04` The game was filled with bugs and

<a id="L708"></a>**L0708** `00:28:06` problems from the start. For example,

<a id="L709"></a>**L0709** `00:28:08` servers were peer-to-peer, which led to

<a id="L710"></a>**L0710** `00:28:10` widespread connection issues. Even

<a id="L711"></a>**L0711** `00:28:12` Crocat made a video addressing these

<a id="L712"></a>**L0712** `00:28:14` problems, garnering over a million

<a id="L713"></a>**L0713** `00:28:15` views. You would get disconnected every

<a id="L714"></a>**L0714** `00:28:17` fourth game and sometimes it made the

<a id="L715"></a>**L0715** `00:28:19` game outright unplayable. Combined with

<a id="L716"></a>**L0716** `00:28:21` a very non-conservative way this

<a id="L717"></a>**L0717** `00:28:23` fighting game worked and a general lack

<a id="L718"></a>**L0718** `00:28:25` of interest from the mainstream for such

<a id="L719"></a>**L0719** `00:28:27` games led to For Honor crashing hard

<a id="L720"></a>**L0720** `00:28:29` after just a few weeks from release,

<a id="L721"></a>**L0721** `00:28:31` losing around 90% of the player base

<a id="L722"></a>**L0722** `00:28:34` with the future of the game being

<a id="L723"></a>**L0723** `00:28:35` uncertain. However, Ubisoft decided to

<a id="L724"></a>**L0724** `00:28:38` stick to the game despite the crash and

<a id="L725"></a>**L0725** `00:28:40` build a cult following that plays the

<a id="L726"></a>**L0726** `00:28:43` game to this day. If you're watching

<a id="L727"></a>**L0727** `00:28:44` this video, it's more likely you have

<a id="L728"></a>**L0728** `00:28:46` been playing for eight years than

<a id="L729"></a>**L0729** `00:28:47` someone who only just started. For Honor

<a id="L730"></a>**L0730** `00:28:49` may not have the largest fan base, but

<a id="L731"></a>**L0731** `00:28:51` definitely one of the most loyal ones.

<a id="L732"></a>**L0732** `00:28:53` There simply is no alternative to this

<a id="L733"></a>**L0733** `00:28:56` game. Besides season 2,

<a id="L734"></a>**L0734** `00:29:01` Apolon was dead. The war had resumed in

<a id="L735"></a>**L0735** `00:29:04` full, and the knights were painfully

<a id="L736"></a>**L0736** `00:29:06` aware that they were unprepared. Ashfeld

<a id="L737"></a>**L0737** `00:29:09` was still reeling from the reality that

<a id="L738"></a>**L0738** `00:29:11` their legions had all been assimilated

<a id="L739"></a>**L0739** `00:29:13` into the Blackstone Legion. And though

<a id="L740"></a>**L0740** `00:29:15` the Iron Legion had been reformed and

<a id="L741"></a>**L0741** `00:29:17` reforged, it was a shadow of its former

<a id="L742"></a>**L0742** `00:29:19` size and influence. As a result, the

<a id="L743"></a>**L0743** `00:29:21` Ashfellians knew that they needed to

<a id="L744"></a>**L0744** `00:29:23` rebuild, restructure, and prepare. The

<a id="L745"></a>**L0745** `00:29:26` old forges were rekindled and ignited to

<a id="L746"></a>**L0746** `00:29:28` begin building armor, weapons, and

<a id="L747"></a>**L0747** `00:29:30` machines of war. But armies, no matter

<a id="L748"></a>**L0748** `00:29:33` how wellarmed, are nothing without

<a id="L749"></a>**L0749** `00:29:35` structure and discipline. And thus the

<a id="L750"></a>**L0750** `00:29:37` knights were blessed with an unlikely

<a id="L751"></a>**L0751** `00:29:39` ally. Echoed from a past they'd long

<a id="L752"></a>**L0752** `00:29:42` thought lost, the Centurions arrived in

<a id="L753"></a>**L0753** `00:29:44` Ashefeld. Armed with a gladius and their

<a id="L754"></a>**L0754** `00:29:47` raw physical might, the Centurions are

<a id="L755"></a>**L0755** `00:29:49` effective fighters in their own right,

<a id="L756"></a>**L0756** `00:29:50` but are even more efficient military

<a id="L757"></a>**L0757** `00:29:52` commanders, leaders, tacticians, and

<a id="L758"></a>**L0758** `00:29:55` trainers. With their assistance and

<a id="L759"></a>**L0759** `00:29:57` direction, the centurions, like the

<a id="L760"></a>**L0760** `00:29:59` forges themselves, were molding the

<a id="L761"></a>**L0761** `00:30:00` weakened and green Ashefeld knight

<a id="L762"></a>**L0762** `00:30:02` legions into fighting forces to be

<a id="L763"></a>**L0763** `00:30:05` reckoned with once again. Meanwhile, the

<a id="L764"></a>**L0764** `00:30:07` samurai were also woefully underprepared

<a id="L765"></a>**L0765** `00:30:10` for the war they were about to wage.

<a id="L766"></a>**L0766** `00:30:12` Years of isolation within their meer had

<a id="L767"></a>**L0767** `00:30:14` made them somewhat ignorant to the

<a id="L768"></a>**L0768** `00:30:16` strength, reach, and capabilities of

<a id="L769"></a>**L0769** `00:30:18` their enemies. With no emperor and their

<a id="L770"></a>**L0770** `00:30:20` information network in pieces, they

<a id="L771"></a>**L0771** `00:30:22` needed information. They needed spies.

<a id="L772"></a>**L0772** `00:30:25` They needed the shadows. And from those

<a id="L773"></a>**L0773** `00:30:28` very shadows emerged the Shinobi, the

<a id="L774"></a>**L0774** `00:30:30` ancient and elite clans of warriors and

<a id="L1100"></a>**L1100** `00:42:29` This season added another two heroes,

<a id="L1101"></a>**L1101** `00:42:31` two new maps, and a rank mode beta that

<a id="L1102"></a>**L1102** `00:42:34` to this day is still in its beta. The

<a id="L1103"></a>**L1103** `00:42:37` first new hero we got is Highlander, a

<a id="L1104"></a>**L1104** `00:42:39` Scottish man with a huge sword joining

<a id="L1105"></a>**L1105** `00:42:41` the Vikings. To this day, the most

<a id="L1107"></a>**L1107** `00:42:45` game with his two modes of fighting.

<a id="L1108"></a>**L1108** `00:42:47` Since release, he has always been

<a id="L1109"></a>**L1109** `00:42:49` considered a weak hero and in the lower

<a id="L1110"></a>**L1110** `00:42:51` half of the tier list. So, Highlanders

<a id="L1111"></a>**L1111** `00:42:53` had to be like super defensive back then

<a id="L1112"></a>**L1112** `00:42:55` and try and just like bait your opponent

<a id="L1113"></a>**L1113** `00:42:57` to throw something so you could in turn

<a id="L1114"></a>**L1114** `00:42:58` CC it and be in your offense. But even

<a id="L1115"></a>**L1115** `00:43:00` then, you stood the risk of getting

<a id="L1116"></a>**L1116** `00:43:02` light parried yourself. A lot of people

<a id="L1117"></a>**L1117** `00:43:04` had mixed reviews of him because he was

<a id="L1118"></a>**L1118** `00:43:05` like super complicated. He was like the

<a id="L1119"></a>**L1119** `00:43:08` only one who takes skill, but back then

<a id="L1120"></a>**L1120** `00:43:10` like he he was just really bad and like

<a id="L1121"></a>**L1121** `00:43:13` you could just sit on Lawbringer turtle

<a id="L1122"></a>**L1122** `00:43:16` the entire time, fight Highlander and

<a id="L1123"></a>**L1123** `00:43:18` there wasn't really much you could do as

<a id="L1124"></a>**L1124** `00:43:20` the Highlander. While the character

<a id="L1125"></a>**L1125** `00:43:22` isn't very popular, he has grown a niche

<a id="L1126"></a>**L1126** `00:43:24` cult following of max reputation

<a id="L1127"></a>**L1127** `00:43:26` highlander mains, some of whom even

<a id="L1128"></a>**L1128** `00:43:28` being the best duelists in the game. The

<a id="L1129"></a>**L1129** `00:43:30` second new hero is Gladiator. One thing

<a id="L1130"></a>**L1130** `00:43:31` to know that I haven't brought up yet is

<a id="L1131"></a>**L1131** `00:43:33` how stamina worked back then. When you

<a id="L1132"></a>**L1132** `00:43:35` were out of stamina, the enemy could

<a id="L1133"></a>**L1133** `00:43:36` still drain your stamina by bashing or

<a id="L1134"></a>**L1134** `00:43:38` throwing you, and you wouldn't

<a id="L1135"></a>**L1135** `00:43:39` regenerate your stamina by falling down.

<a id="L1136"></a>**L1136** `00:43:41` This meant that going out of stamina was

<a id="L1137"></a>**L1137** `00:43:43` extremely dangerous, and you usually

<a id="L1138"></a>**L1138** `00:43:45` wouldn't have the time to get your

<a id="L1139"></a>**L1139** `00:43:46` stamina back. Some characters had moves

<a id="L1140"></a>**L1140** `00:43:48` that would quickly drain the entire

<a id="L1141"></a>**L1141** `00:43:49` stamina of the opponent. for example,

<a id="L1142"></a>**L1142** `00:43:51` Centurion, giving them the title of

<a id="L1143"></a>**L1143** `00:43:53` stamina bullies. Gladiator was another

<a id="L1144"></a>**L1144** `00:43:55` one of these stamina bullies. When an

<a id="L1145"></a>**L1145** `00:43:56` enemy was out of stamina, Gladiator's

<a id="L1146"></a>**L1146** `00:43:58` toaster would guarantee a heavy. He

<a id="L1147"></a>**L1147** `00:44:00` could repeat this many times if they get

<a id="L1148"></a>**L1148** `00:44:02` out of stamina just once. This is an

<a id="L1149"></a>**L1149** `00:44:04` issue that would stay in FA for many

<a id="L1150"></a>**L1150** `00:44:06` years. Entire play styles were built

<a id="L1151"></a>**L1151** `00:44:09` around this. They finally got rid of

<a id="L1152"></a>**L1152** `00:44:11` Nubush's out of Lo tech, but the damage

<a id="L1153"></a>**L1153** `00:44:13` it did to Fona's reputation cannot be

<a id="L1154"></a>**L1154** `00:44:15` undone. They also increased the max

<a id="L1155"></a>**L1155** `00:44:18` reputation rate from 30 to 40. At the

<a id="L1156"></a>**L1156** `00:44:20` same time, people discover just how

<a id="L1214"></a>**L1214** `00:46:25` The samurai and the Vikings were both

<a id="L1215"></a>**L1215** `00:46:27` putting up valiant struggles against the

<a id="L1216"></a>**L1216** `00:46:29` knights, but the raw military strength

<a id="L1217"></a>**L1217** `00:46:31` the knights were able to field was still

<a id="L1218"></a>**L1218** `00:46:32` overwhelming. So, the two factions

<a id="L1219"></a>**L1219** `00:46:35` decided to turn to their more desperate

<a id="L1220"></a>**L1220** `00:46:37` allies, if such a term can even apply to

<a id="L1221"></a>**L1221** `00:46:39` them. The samurai looked to the

<a id="L1222"></a>**L1222** `00:46:42` masterless samurai who had been released

<a id="L1223"></a>**L1223** `00:46:44` from their servitude to their masters.

<a id="L1224"></a>**L1224** `00:46:46` Wielding twin katana, the swear no

<a id="L1225"></a>**L1225** `00:46:48` loyalty to anyone but themselves and

<a id="L1226"></a>**L1226** `00:46:51` their own destinies. But now that the

<a id="L1227"></a>**L1227** `00:46:53` Meyers's need is greatest, they might

<a id="L1228"></a>**L1228** `00:46:55` now turn their blades on the foes of the

<a id="L1229"></a>**L1229** `00:46:57` samurai rather than the masters who once

<a id="L1230"></a>**L1230** `00:46:59` scorned them. Meanwhile, the Vikings

<a id="L1231"></a>**L1231** `00:47:01` turn to the shamans, wild, feral, and

<a id="L1232"></a>**L1232** `00:47:04` savage women of the land who commune

<a id="L1233"></a>**L1233** `00:47:06` with the spirits of nature, with the

<a id="L1234"></a>**L1234** `00:47:08` gods, and with the dead. As ready to

<a id="L1235"></a>**L1235** `00:47:10` lash out savagely upon her own allies as

<a id="L1236"></a>**L1236** `00:47:12` her enemies, the shamans fight with

<a id="L1237"></a>**L1237** `00:47:14` dagger and hatchet, and even their own

<a id="L1238"></a>**L1238** `00:47:16` teeth, proving to be unpredictable and

<a id="L1239"></a>**L1239** `00:47:18` ferocious adversaries. These wild cards

<a id="L1240"></a>**L1240** `00:47:21` were the key to tipping the balance. And

<a id="L1241"></a>**L1241** `00:47:23` at long last, the knights were unable to

<a id="L1242"></a>**L1242** `00:47:26` fully break the samurai and Vikings,

<a id="L1243"></a>**L1243** `00:47:28` leading to something of a stalemate. As

<a id="L1244"></a>**L1244** `00:47:32` they learned that loyalty was a fickle

<a id="L1245"></a>**L1245** `00:47:34` matter, and that you had to widen your

<a id="L1246"></a>**L1246** `00:47:36` trust to win this war, now the knights

<a id="L1247"></a>**L1247** `00:47:38` had no choice but to face those fiery

<a id="L1248"></a>**L1248** `00:47:41` and rebellious newcomers that were

<a id="L1249"></a>**L1249** `00:47:42` walking a fine line between order and

<a id="L1250"></a>**L1250** `00:47:46` havoc.

<a id="L1251"></a>**L1251** `00:47:48` Shaman was easily one of the most hated

<a id="L1252"></a>**L1252** `00:47:50` characters on launch. When I got my

<a id="L1253"></a>**L1253** `00:47:52` hands on this class, I was given early

<a id="L1254"></a>**L1254** `00:47:54` access to test it out a couple weeks

<a id="L1255"></a>**L1255** `00:47:56` early. I was testing it against other

<a id="L1256"></a>**L1256** `00:47:58` creators, and we all simultaneously

<a id="L1257"></a>**L1257** `00:48:00` agreed that this class was going to be a

<a id="L1258"></a>**L1258** `00:48:03` huge problem on release. The reason why

<a id="L1259"></a>**L1259** `00:48:05` this class was so disliked is very

<a id="L1260"></a>**L1260** `00:48:07` specifically the overpowering damage

<a id="L1261"></a>**L1261** `00:48:10` that you can deal in such a short amount

<a id="L1262"></a>**L1262** `00:48:12` of time. The class had an insanely

<a id="L1263"></a>**L1263** `00:48:14` strong ranged auto attack that you could

<a id="L1264"></a>**L1264** `00:48:18` not avoid. Its bleeds were devastating.

<a id="L1265"></a>**L1265** `00:48:20` It had a bleed follow-up where you could

<a id="L1266"></a>**L1266** `00:48:22` jump and bite the class, giving you full

<a id="L1267"></a>**L1267** `00:48:24` stamina back, damaging half the HP bar

<a id="L1268"></a>**L1268** `00:48:27` of the enemy. You could do a one-shot

<a id="L1269"></a>**L1269** `00:48:29` combo within 2 seconds. And most of the

<a id="L1270"></a>**L1270** `00:48:31` time, people did not know how to react

<a id="L1271"></a>**L1271** `00:48:32` cuz it had an unblockable. It had a

<a id="L1272"></a>**L1272** `00:48:34` dodge attack. It had a deflect into

<a id="L1273"></a>**L1273** `00:48:37` bleed. There was anything you could

<a id="L1274"></a>**L1274** `00:48:39` think of, this class had it. It was

<a id="L1275"></a>**L1275** `00:48:41` proven evidently so when I started

<a id="L1276"></a>**L1276** `00:48:43` uploading videos showcasing it and

<a id="L1277"></a>**L1277** `00:48:45` getting tons and tons of dislikes and

<a id="L1278"></a>**L1278** `00:48:48` hateful comments. The class had counters

<a id="L1279"></a>**L1279** `00:48:50` though, despite how strong it was. And

<a id="L1280"></a>**L1280** `00:48:52` slowly over time, people started to

<a id="L1281"></a>**L1281** `00:48:54` realize, yes, this is a higher tier

<a id="L1282"></a>**L1282** `00:48:56` class, but it's definitely not

<a id="L1314"></a>**L1314** `00:50:02` Tribute was basically capture the flag

<a id="L1382"></a>**L1382** `00:52:36` I'm playing for honor since the open

<a id="L1383"></a>**L1383** `00:52:38` beta and I was asked by my friend Garen

<a id="L1384"></a>**L1384** `00:52:40` because I'm a peacekeeper man over 9

<a id="L1385"></a>**L1385** `00:52:42` years. When for honor was released,

<a id="L1386"></a>**L1386** `00:52:44` peacekeeper kit was light, heavies, zone

<a id="L1387"></a>**L1387** `00:52:47` attack, dodge attack and defense. But

<a id="L1388"></a>**L1388** `00:52:49` what made her unique was her gun break

<a id="L1389"></a>**L1389** `00:52:51` punish and her bleed damage. Peacekeeper

<a id="L1390"></a>**L1390** `00:52:53` could just light everybody and win. Zone

<a id="L1391"></a>**L1391** `00:52:55` attack was also super fast and pretty

<a id="L1392"></a>**L1392** `00:52:57` much unreactable for most people.

<a id="L1393"></a>**L1393** `00:52:59` Problem with her zone though, you could

<a id="L1394"></a>**L1394** `00:53:01` just use it like two times before you

<a id="L1395"></a>**L1395** `00:53:03` were out of stamina. Her damage was

<a id="L1396"></a>**L1396** `00:53:05` really good as well. I remember that a

<a id="L1397"></a>**L1397** `00:53:07` triple step guard breakage take almost

<a id="L1398"></a>**L1398** `00:53:09` half your opponent's health at that

<a id="L1399"></a>**L1399** `00:53:11` time. Getting the third step on guard

<a id="L1400"></a>**L1400** `00:53:13` break was actually pretty difficult

<a id="L1401"></a>**L1401** `00:53:15` because the timing of step was really

<a id="L1402"></a>**L1402** `00:53:17` strict and you actually needed to

<a id="L1403"></a>**L1403** `00:53:19` practice to be able to get all three

<a id="L1404"></a>**L1404** `00:53:20` stacks. Other part of her kit was her

<a id="L1405"></a>**L1405** `00:53:22` dagger cancels which at the time were

<a id="L1406"></a>**L1406** `00:53:24` pretty useless. Their dagger castles

<a id="L1407"></a>**L1407** `00:53:26` were coming from the same side as the

<a id="L1408"></a>**L1408** `00:53:28` opener heavy. They could easily block it

<a id="L1409"></a>**L1409** `00:53:31` and if they decided to parry, they could

<a id="L1410"></a>**L1410** `00:53:33` parry both heavy and dagger castle on

<a id="L1411"></a>**L1411** `00:53:35` the same time. Despite this, she was at

<a id="L1412"></a>**L1412** `00:53:37` least in my opinion one of the strongest

<a id="L1413"></a>**L1413** `00:53:39` hero in the game. But as the time went

<a id="L1414"></a>**L1414** `00:53:41` by, people learned how to parry and how

<a id="L1415"></a>**L1415** `00:53:43` to react better to the lights. Pretty

<a id="L1416"></a>**L1416** `00:53:45` soon, they were able to block or parry

<a id="L1417"></a>**L1417** `00:53:47` her lights pretty consistently. Luckily

<a id="L1418"></a>**L1418** `00:53:49` for peacekeeper, people still didn't

<a id="L1419"></a>**L1419** `00:53:51` know how to differentiate the light and

<a id="L1420"></a>**L1420** `00:53:53` heavies. So, they were basically

<a id="L1421"></a>**L1421** `00:53:54` carrying a red, so you could catch them

<a id="L1422"></a>**L1422** `00:53:56` with heavies. I still remember this one

<a id="L1423"></a>**L1423** `00:53:58` match when I was only throwing heavies

<a id="L1424"></a>**L1424** `00:54:00` and my opponent was trying to light and

<a id="L1425"></a>**L1425** `00:54:02` the heavy always landed and I managed to

<a id="L1426"></a>**L1426** `00:54:04` defeat him by just using heavies. The

<a id="L1427"></a>**L1427** `00:54:06` tiny thing was that he actually called

<a id="L1428"></a>**L1428** `00:54:08` me a heavy spell. I think that is about

<a id="L1429"></a>**L1429** `00:54:10` everything I could say about Beast

<a id="L1430"></a>**L1430** `00:54:12` Keeper. After 9 years, she's still my

<a id="L1431"></a>**L1431** `00:54:14` favorite hero. I still have a lot of fun

<a id="L1432"></a>**L1432** `00:54:16` playing her even though I play other

<a id="L1433"></a>**L1433** `00:54:18` heroes with all this considerably. And

<a id="L1434"></a>**L1434** `00:54:20` finally, I would like to thank Garrison

<a id="L1435"></a>**L1435** `00:54:22` for inviting me to be part of this

<a id="L1436"></a>**L1436** `00:54:24` ambitious project. So, farewell and see

<a id="L1437"></a>**L1437** `00:54:27` you on the battlefield.

<a id="L1438"></a>**L1438** `00:54:29` >> Though Apolon was now long dead, her

<a id="L1439"></a>**L1439** `00:54:32` legacy was cemented and her will made

<a id="L1440"></a>**L1440** `00:54:34` manifest. The factions were now

<a id="L1441"></a>**L1441** `00:54:36` embroiled in an intense and deadlock

<a id="L1442"></a>**L1442** `00:54:38` struggle for power and dominance. The

<a id="L1443"></a>**L1443** `00:54:41` strong rose up and the weak were left by

<a id="L1444"></a>**L1444** `00:54:44` the wayside. All had occurred just as

<a id="L1445"></a>**L1445** `00:54:47` Apolon had desired. She had truly

<a id="L1446"></a>**L1446** `00:54:49` ushered in an age of wolves.

<a id="L1447"></a>**L1447** `00:54:56` Season 5, Age of Wolves, released on

<a id="L1448"></a>**L1448** `00:54:59` February 15th, 2018, and surprised

<a id="L1449"></a>**L1449** `00:55:01` everyone by not adding any new heroes,

<a id="L1450"></a>**L1450** `00:55:04` but instead reworking old heroes and

<a id="L1451"></a>**L1451** `00:55:06` improving the quality of life for the

<a id="L1452"></a>**L1452** `00:55:08` game. I remember I was such a kid like I

<a id="L1453"></a>**L1453** `00:55:10` was on the Toys R Us. We had three

<a id="L1454"></a>**L1454** `00:55:12` different reworks. We had balancing

<a id="L1455"></a>**L1455** `00:55:15` patches for almost all heroes. We had

<a id="L1456"></a>**L1456** `00:55:18` mechanic art of war mechanic changes. It

<a id="L1457"></a>**L1457** `00:55:21` was like kids on a candy store. Okay. It

<a id="L1458"></a>**L1458** `00:55:25` was such a massive update the patch outs

<a id="L1459"></a>**L1459** `00:55:27` would take 50 minutes to read and

<a id="L1460"></a>**L1460** `00:55:29` included three full-on reworks for Ken,

<a id="L1461"></a>**L1461** `00:55:32` Berserker, and one more that we'll get

<a id="L1462"></a>**L1462** `00:55:34` on in a bit. Berserker's changes were

<a id="L1463"></a>**L1463** `00:55:36` mostly in his properties. He had no

<a id="L1464"></a>**L1464** `00:55:38` constant hyper armor during his infinite

<a id="L1465"></a>**L1465** `00:55:40` chain, and his light attacks were one of

<a id="L1466"></a>**L1466** `00:55:42` the first to be turned to 400

<a id="L1467"></a>**L1467** `00:55:44` milliseconds. Furthermore, his chain top

<a id="L1468"></a>**L1468** `00:55:46` heavy is now unblockable, and he

<a id="L1469"></a>**L1469** `00:55:48` received some more minor tweaks here and

<a id="L1470"></a>**L1470** `00:55:49` there. K's rework in season 5 is to this

<a id="L1471"></a>**L1471** `00:55:52` day regarded as the best rework in For

<a id="L1472"></a>**L1472** `00:55:55` Honor history. His top heavies went from

<a id="L1473"></a>**L1473** `00:55:58` being super fast to super slow, but with

<a id="L1474"></a>**L1474** `00:56:00` a huge amount of options to go by. To go

<a id="L1475"></a>**L1475** `00:56:03` with this, his unblockables faint much

<a id="L1476"></a>**L1476** `00:56:05` later now, making it unreactable. His

<a id="L1477"></a>**L1477** `00:56:08` third side heavies and knights have

<a id="L1478"></a>**L1478** `00:56:09` instant high armor. Ker got a forward

<a id="L1479"></a>**L1479** `00:56:11` sleep attack. His zone was replaced by a

<a id="L1480"></a>**L1480** `00:56:14` super fast one, and he even received a

<a id="L1481"></a>**L1481** `00:56:16` small bash from his top heavy. His

<a id="L1482"></a>**L1482** `00:56:18` animations also became much smoother in

<a id="L1483"></a>**L1483** `00:56:19` the meantime. Overall, the new caner

<a id="L1484"></a>**L1484** `00:56:21` manages to stay true to the character

<a id="L1485"></a>**L1485** `00:56:23` while also improving everything towards

<a id="L1486"></a>**L1486** `00:56:26` the can play style. But this sadly

<a id="L1487"></a>**L1487** `00:56:28` didn't stop many Kent players to just

<a id="L1488"></a>**L1488** `00:56:30` spam dodge tack with him. Cancer main

<a id="L1489"></a>**L1489** `00:56:31` veins really are either the most

<a id="L1490"></a>**L1490** `00:56:33` skillful players you'll ever meet or the

<a id="L1491"></a>**L1491** `00:56:35` stupidest, no in between.

<a id="L1492"></a>**L1492** `00:56:36` >> It was the same mechanic with like

<a id="L1493"></a>**L1493** `00:56:38` Highlander and Shaman with Kins soft

<a id="L1494"></a>**L1494** `00:56:41` where you couldn't really do much

<a id="L1495"></a>**L1495** `00:56:42` against him. And his fourth feet, he's

<a id="L1496"></a>**L1496** `00:56:44` like practically unparable and the only

<a id="L1497"></a>**L1497** `00:56:46` way to like really get around him was

<a id="L1498"></a>**L1498** `00:56:48` wait for him to make a mistake that you

<a id="L1499"></a>**L1499** `00:56:50` could get around and just counter him.

<a id="L1500"></a>**L1500** `00:56:53` We saw a Polyon on the trailer and we

<a id="L1501"></a>**L1501** `00:56:56` didn't really know what it was at the

<a id="L1502"></a>**L1502** `00:56:58` time. When the event dropped and we saw

<a id="L1503"></a>**L1503** `00:57:00` that event game mode, we were like,

<a id="L1505"></a>**L1505** `00:57:03` out." Did not realize we were going to

<a id="L1506"></a>**L1506** `00:57:06` be squaring up against four really

<a id="L1507"></a>**L1507** `00:57:09` stacked bosses

<a id="L1508"></a>**L1508** `00:57:12` from the campaign. Like, it was pretty

<a id="L1509"></a>**L1509** `00:57:14` badass. And uh the rewards for the event

<a id="L1510"></a>**L1510** `00:57:17` were pretty cool, too. That uh Dark One

<a id="L1511"></a>**L1511** `00:57:20` came out for this event, which was

<a id="L1512"></a>**L1512** `00:57:21` freaking sweet. the Apollium move set

<a id="L1513"></a>**L1513** `00:57:24` was just so wild at some points and then

<a id="L1517"></a>**L1517** `00:57:34` never, you know, a guaranteed win every

<a id="L1518"></a>**L1518** `00:57:37` time. We definitely lost quite a bit.

<a id="L1519"></a>**L1519** `00:57:38` What was that other That's right. Yeah,

<a id="L1520"></a>**L1520** `00:57:40` there was a a Orochi boss in that event

<a id="L1521"></a>**L1521** `00:57:45` and he definitely beat my ass. It was so

<a id="L1522"></a>**L1522** `00:57:49` annoying.

<a id="L1523"></a>**L1523** `00:57:52` Dedicated servers are something every

<a id="L1524"></a>**L1524** `00:57:54` game needs to strive for because

<a id="L1525"></a>**L1525** `00:57:56` peerto-peer is something we've left in

<a id="L1526"></a>**L1526** `00:57:57` the past. Basically, when we had Call of

<a id="L1527"></a>**L1527** `00:57:59` Duty and peerto-peer existed, we had

<a id="L1528"></a>**L1528** `00:58:01` things like host advantage, which could

<a id="L1529"></a>**L1529** `00:58:03` really make games unbalanced towards

<a id="L1530"></a>**L1530** `00:58:06` anyone that wasn't host. Froner launched

<a id="L1531"></a>**L1531** `00:58:09` with peer-to-peer and that already saw

<a id="L1532"></a>**L1532** `00:58:12` negative effects basically immediately.

<a id="L1533"></a>**L1533** `00:58:15` Now, where the negative effects came

<a id="L1534"></a>**L1534** `00:58:16` from the host migrations all the time,

<a id="L1535"></a>**L1535** `00:58:18` the pauses and stats, and just overall

<a id="L1536"></a>**L1536** `00:58:20` bad latency between everyone except the

<a id="L1537"></a>**L1537** `00:58:23` host. Now, finally, after an entire year

<a id="L1538"></a>**L1538** `00:58:25` went by, they finally made the switch to

<a id="L1539"></a>**L1539** `00:58:27` dedicated servers. Now, if I remember

<a id="L1540"></a>**L1540** `00:58:29` correctly, it wasn't a very smooth

<a id="L1541"></a>**L1541** `00:58:30` switch. It took its time to be fleshed

<a id="L1542"></a>**L1542** `00:58:33` out and done properly, but it's still

<a id="L1543"></a>**L1543** `00:58:35` good. Switching to dedicated servers is

<a id="L1544"></a>**L1544** `00:58:37` always something games need to go for

<a id="L1545"></a>**L1545** `00:58:38` because even in games now when they have

<a id="L1783"></a>**L1783** `01:08:09` mix. Of course, there was also flicker

<a id="L1784"></a>**L1784** `01:08:11` tech with Orochi where he would throw a

<a id="L1785"></a>**L1785** `01:08:12` top light but it flicker to his own.

<a id="L1786"></a>**L1786** `01:08:14` That's pretty iconic. There used to be a

<a id="L1787"></a>**L1787** `01:08:16` tech with the Ruchi where you can do a

<a id="L1788"></a>**L1788** `01:08:18` 180 to 360 dodge light but that's not in

<a id="L1789"></a>**L1789** `01:08:20` the game anymore unfortunately. There's

<a id="L1790"></a>**L1790** `01:08:21` another thing I call sephro tech where

<a id="L1791"></a>**L1791** `01:08:23` you can hit someone and they're not even

<a id="L1792"></a>**L1792** `01:08:25` near you with the top heavy. There's a

<a id="L1793"></a>**L1793** `01:08:26` couple things I figured out.

<a id="L1794"></a>**L1794** `01:08:32` So there are three factions, knights,

<a id="L1795"></a>**L1795** `01:08:34` Vikings, and the samurai. Part most

<a id="L1796"></a>**L1796** `01:08:36` people thought that the factions would

<a id="L1797"></a>**L1797** `01:08:37` work that way where if you were to pick

<a id="L1798"></a>**L1798** `01:08:39` one specific type of faction, you were

<a id="L1799"></a>**L1799** `01:08:41` only able to play characters from that

<a id="L1800"></a>**L1800** `01:08:44` said faction. How they ended up working

<a id="L1801"></a>**L1801** `01:08:46` was didn't matter what type of faction

<a id="L1802"></a>**L1802** `01:08:49` you would pick, you were still able to

<a id="L1803"></a>**L1803** `01:08:51` play whatever characters from any

<a id="L1804"></a>**L1804** `01:08:53` faction that you would like to play. You

<a id="L1805"></a>**L1805** `01:08:56` would deploy troops on a map and the

<a id="L1806"></a>**L1806** `01:08:58` only way to do that is by playing

<a id="L1807"></a>**L1807** `01:09:00` matches. But it came to a point where

<a id="L1808"></a>**L1808** `01:09:03` the Vikings were winning everything. And

<a id="L1809"></a>**L1809** `01:09:06` people were like, "Dude, I might as well

<a id="L1810"></a>**L1810** `01:09:08` just join the Vikings. It doesn't matter

<a id="L1811"></a>**L1811** `01:09:10` what faction I choose because there's no

<a id="L1812"></a>**L1812** `01:09:12` drawback. There's no punishment for

<a id="L1813"></a>**L1813** `01:09:14` changing into a new faction as well."

<a id="L1814"></a>**L1814** `01:09:16` So, everyone started just going to

<a id="L1815"></a>**L1815** `01:09:18` Vikings, which is understandable. And

<a id="L1816"></a>**L1816** `01:09:21` then also, the Knights were just focused

<a id="L1817"></a>**L1817** `01:09:22` on the volcanoes. It's like the volcano

<a id="L1818"></a>**L1818** `01:09:25` part of the map for whatever reason.

<a id="L1819"></a>**L1819** `01:09:27` They called it the uh vault volcano. It

<a id="L1820"></a>**L1820** `01:09:30` It was kind of weird. The samurai was

<a id="L1821"></a>**L1821** `01:09:32` the worst faction by far in honor.

<a id="L1822"></a>**L1822** `01:09:34` Everyone who was on samurai, rest in

<a id="L1823"></a>**L1823** `01:09:37` peace cuz that was the worst faction.

<a id="L1824"></a>**L1824** `01:09:40` And yeah, I think that I think that

<a id="L1825"></a>**L1825** `01:09:43` pretty much covers it.

<a id="L1826"></a>**L1826** `01:09:47` Perhaps war itself has an impact on the

<a id="L1827"></a>**L1827** `01:09:50` balance of nature. For without warning,

<a id="L1828"></a>**L1828** `01:09:52` the volcano Mount Ignis suddenly erupted

<a id="L1829"></a>**L1829** `01:09:55` in a catastrophic explosion of smoke,

<a id="L1830"></a>**L1830** `01:09:57` ash, and magma. Fire rained from the

<a id="L1831"></a>**L1831** `01:10:00` sky, and the sun was shrouded in a veil

<a id="L1832"></a>**L1832** `01:10:02` of smog and thick, noxious clouds. Crops

<a id="L1833"></a>**L1833** `01:10:06` withered and died. Fields burned, rivers

<a id="L1834"></a>**L1834** `01:10:08` of magma cut through towns and villages,

<a id="L1835"></a>**L1835** `01:10:11` and all three of the factions were

<a id="L1836"></a>**L1836** `01:10:13` forced to retreat from the mountains for

<a id="L1837"></a>**L1837** `01:10:15` safety. But if the heat of the volcano

<a id="L1838"></a>**L1838** `01:10:17` was mighty, the passion of the waring

<a id="L1839"></a>**L1839** `01:10:20` groups burned hotter still. Taking heart

<a id="L1840"></a>**L1840** `01:10:22` and even encouragement from this

<a id="L1841"></a>**L1841** `01:10:24` eruption, the factions all took it as an

<a id="L1842"></a>**L1842** `01:10:26` omen of what they weren't quite sure.

<a id="L1843"></a>**L1843** `01:10:28` But what they were sure about was that

<a id="L1844"></a>**L1844** `01:10:30` they would redouble their efforts for

<a id="L1845"></a>**L1845** `01:10:31` victory. So in the shade of fiery death,

<a id="L1846"></a>**L1846** `01:10:35` the war waged on in storms and fury.

<a id="L1865"></a>**L1865** `01:11:19` attack, an unblockable top heavy, and

<a id="L1866"></a>**L1866** `01:11:21` most importantly, his shoulder bash was

<a id="L1867"></a>**L1867** `01:11:23` buffed. If you dodge the short bash, the

<a id="L1868"></a>**L1868** `01:11:26` long bash will always hit you unless you

<a id="L1869"></a>**L1869** `01:11:28` roll away. The warden that we know of

<a id="L1870"></a>**L1870** `01:11:30` today is taking shape. Silver matter on

<a id="L1871"></a>**L1871** `01:11:32` Warden, boys. Warden gets his rework and

<a id="L1872"></a>**L1872** `01:11:36` it actually goes slightly down because

<a id="L1873"></a>**L1873** `01:11:38` it's not as cringy of a character and as

<a id="L1874"></a>**L1874** `01:11:40` unreactable with the flying animations,

<a id="L1875"></a>**L1875** `01:11:43` not broken as

<a id="L1876"></a>**L1876** `01:11:44` >> in year 2, we saw consistently returning

<a id="L1877"></a>**L1877** `01:11:47` game mode called Carousel of Death,

<a id="L1878"></a>**L1878** `01:11:49` which quickly became a fan favorite of

<a id="L1879"></a>**L1879** `01:11:52` event game modes.

<a id="L1880"></a>**L1880** `01:11:53` >> It was the first time where we just

<a id="L1881"></a>**L1881** `01:11:55` eight people in a arena just go ape [ __ ]

<a id="L1882"></a>**L1882** `01:11:57` at each other. you know, team with the

<a id="L1883"></a>**L1883** `01:11:58` most standing wins. Keep dying and

<a id="L1884"></a>**L1884** `01:12:01` longer respawns in between each death.

<a id="L1885"></a>**L1885** `01:12:03` And uh it was pretty fun.

<a id="L1886"></a>**L1886** `01:12:06` >> Players started getting really good at

<a id="L1887"></a>**L1887** `01:12:08` the game. Lawbringer was still left in

<a id="L1888"></a>**L1888** `01:12:11` the dust for a very, very, very long

<a id="L1889"></a>**L1889** `01:12:14` time. I wanted to make Lawbringer a

<a id="L1890"></a>**L1890** `01:12:16` competitive class in rank specifically.

<a id="L1891"></a>**L1891** `01:12:20` However, the problem was that most

<a id="L1892"></a>**L1892** `01:12:22` classes have been reworked or new

<a id="L1893"></a>**L1893** `01:12:24` classes were built in with faster

<a id="L1894"></a>**L1894** `01:12:26` attacks and new tech. The Lawbringer was

<a id="L1895"></a>**L1895** `01:12:29` left behind as a season 1 relic, still

<a id="L1896"></a>**L1896** `01:12:32` waiting for its long overdue rework. But

<a id="L1897"></a>**L1897** `01:12:35` until then, I wanted to make it viable.

<a id="L1898"></a>**L1898** `01:12:38` So, I came up with something called the

<a id="L1899"></a>**L1899** `01:12:40` kit strat. The kite strat was a way to

<a id="L1900"></a>**L1900** `01:12:42` make the class viable in ranked. And how

<a id="L1901"></a>**L1901** `01:12:45` is that possible? The moment you're

<a id="L1902"></a>**L1902** `01:12:46` staying locked on on an enemy, you can't

<a id="L1903"></a>**L1903** `01:12:48` do anything with lawbringer against high

<a id="L1904"></a>**L1904** `01:12:50` top level players. They will react to

<a id="L1905"></a>**L1905** `01:12:53` everything the lawbringer has to throw

<a id="L1906"></a>**L1906** `01:12:55` out at you. Because the lawbringer was

<a id="L1907"></a>**L1907** `01:12:56` so slow, I decided to change it up. And

<a id="L1908"></a>**L1908** `01:12:59` instead of being defensive with it, I

<a id="L1909"></a>**L1909** `01:13:01` needed to be offensive. Instead of just

<a id="L1910"></a>**L1910** `01:13:02` throwing out attacks, I baited the

<a id="L1911"></a>**L1911** `01:13:04` enemy's attacks by unlocking and walking

<a id="L1912"></a>**L1912** `01:13:07` towards my screen, but moving in a way

<a id="L1913"></a>**L1913** `01:13:09` that made it look like I was trying to

<a id="L1914"></a>**L1914** `01:13:11` run away. Instead, what I was actually

<a id="L1915"></a>**L1915** `01:13:13` doing was watching their moves, making

<a id="L1916"></a>**L1916** `01:13:16` them do a very predictable running

<a id="L1917"></a>**L1917** `01:13:17` attack or jump attack. And when they do

<a id="L1918"></a>**L1918** `01:13:19` that, it's a very easy tell, as long as

<a id="L1919"></a>**L1919** `01:13:22` you're locked on, to parry it and

<a id="L1920"></a>**L1920** `01:13:24` retaliate. This tech was very stupid and

<a id="L1921"></a>**L1921** `01:13:27` I regret ever making it. But during the

<a id="L1922"></a>**L1922** `01:13:30` time that I did come up with it, it

<a id="L1923"></a>**L1923** `01:13:32` actually worked kind of well. A lot of

<a id="L1924"></a>**L1924** `01:13:34` players became frustrated because in

<a id="L1925"></a>**L1925** `01:13:36` ranked, the winner goes to the person

<a id="L1926"></a>**L1926** `01:13:38` with the most HP. So, as long as you do

<a id="L1927"></a>**L1927** `01:13:40` the damage that needs to be done,

<a id="L1928"></a>**L1928** `01:13:42` they're on the timer to do the damage to

<a id="L1929"></a>**L1929** `01:13:44` you. Tick tock these enemy players makes

<a id="L1930"></a>**L1930** `01:13:46` them very frustrated. They will jump at

<a id="L1931"></a>**L1931** `01:13:48` you at any opportunity that they could,

<a id="L1932"></a>**L1932** `01:13:50` giving you more damage as long as you

<a id="L1933"></a>**L1933** `01:13:52` lock on on time and react accordingly.

<a id="L1934"></a>**L1934** `01:13:55` You are now turning the tide of battle

<a id="L1935"></a>**L1935** `01:13:57` and making the rounds go in your favor.

<a id="L1936"></a>**L1936** `01:13:59` This was a strat that seemed like it was

<a id="L1937"></a>**L1937** `01:14:01` viable. However, at the end of the day,

<a id="L1938"></a>**L1938** `01:14:04` it was really stupid that I had to

<a id="L1939"></a>**L1939** `01:14:06` unlock in the first place to make the

<a id="L1940"></a>**L1940** `01:14:08` class work, and it was very apparent how

<a id="L1941"></a>**L1941** `01:14:11` obvious this class needed a change, and

<a id="L1942"></a>**L1942** `01:14:14` desperately.

<a id="L1943"></a>**L1943** `01:14:17` Far to the east of Heathmore, there once

<a id="L1944"></a>**L1944** `01:14:19` was a spectacular empire ordained and

<a id="L1945"></a>**L1945** `01:14:22` overseen by heaven itself. When the

<a id="L1946"></a>**L1946** `01:14:24` cataclysm struck, the empire collapsed

<a id="L1947"></a>**L1947** `01:14:26` and what followed was an endless civil

<a id="L1948"></a>**L1948** `01:14:28` war between rival warlords and governors

<a id="L1949"></a>**L1949** `01:14:31` for control of the throne. In the end,

<a id="L1950"></a>**L1950** `01:14:33` one of these waring groups decided to

<a id="L1951"></a>**L1951** `01:14:35` expand beyond the borders of their old

<a id="L1952"></a>**L1952** `01:14:37` holdings and explore the lands westward,

<a id="L1953"></a>**L1953** `01:14:39` which led them straight to the

<a id="L1954"></a>**L1954** `01:14:40` unprepared and unaware factions of

<a id="L1955"></a>**L1955** `01:14:43` Heathmore. The knights, samurai, and

<a id="L1956"></a>**L1956** `01:14:45` Vikings were caught blindsided by the

<a id="L1957"></a>**L1957** `01:14:47` arrival of this foreign invading force

<a id="L1958"></a>**L1958** `01:14:49` known as the Wuin. Commanded by their

<a id="L1959"></a>**L1959** `01:14:52` self-proclaimed emperor, Gowle, the

<a id="L1960"></a>**L1960** `01:14:55` Woolin seek to restore the mandate of

<a id="L1961"></a>**L1961** `01:14:57` heaven and find their place in this

<a id="L1962"></a>**L1962** `01:14:58` world as they slaughter and cut down any

<a id="L1963"></a>**L1963** `01:15:01` who oppose their divine calling. Among

<a id="L1964"></a>**L1964** `01:15:04` their number, four notable warriors

<a id="L1965"></a>**L1965** `01:15:06` carve their titles and strength into the

<a id="L1966"></a>**L1966** `01:15:08` hearts of Heithmore. The Tiandi, the

<a id="L1967"></a>**L1967** `01:15:11` vanguard of the Wuin, fighting with Dao

<a id="L1968"></a>**L1968** `01:15:13` swords and bound by oaths of unbreakable

<a id="L1969"></a>**L1969** `01:15:15` loyalty and devotion. They are the

<a id="L1970"></a>**L1970** `01:15:17` blades of the emperor and the defenders

<a id="L1971"></a>**L1971** `01:15:19` of kings and governors. The Jean Jun,

<a id="L1972"></a>**L1972** `01:15:21` the military geniuses of the Wuin, who

<a id="L1973"></a>**L1973** `01:15:24` march into war with a Guandow glaive and

<a id="L1974"></a>**L1974** `01:15:26` with experience to demonstrate their

<a id="L1975"></a>**L1975** `01:15:28` expertise and mastery of war. They

<a id="L1976"></a>**L1976** `01:15:31` command discipline and respect from

<a id="L1977"></a>**L1977** `01:15:33` their soldiers and underlings and will

<a id="L1978"></a>**L1978** `01:15:35` settle for nothing short of victory. The

<a id="L1979"></a>**L1979** `01:15:37` Nucia, elegant dancers and performers

<a id="L1980"></a>**L1980** `01:15:39` whose grace and beauty mask a deadly

<a id="L1981"></a>**L1981** `01:15:41` precision. Assassins with dual hook

<a id="L1982"></a>**L1982** `01:15:43` swords as their instruments. The Nucia

<a id="L1983"></a>**L1983** `01:15:46` dance and weave through enemy ranks,

<a id="L1984"></a>**L1984** `01:15:47` evading attack and slipping right

<a id="L1985"></a>**L1985** `01:15:49` through enemy defenses. And the Shaolin,

<a id="L1986"></a>**L1986** `01:15:52` once monks who practice peace and

<a id="L1987"></a>**L1987** `01:15:54` meditation to reach enlightenment, they

<a id="L1988"></a>**L1988** `01:15:56` now carry their prayers and faith into

<a id="L1989"></a>**L1989** `01:15:58` war, using their bow staffs to deliver

<a id="L1990"></a>**L1990** `01:16:01` their doctrine and their piety at the

<a id="L1991"></a>**L1991** `01:16:03` end of bonebreaking fury. They have lost

<a id="L1992"></a>**L1992** `01:16:05` too much already, and they will not

<a id="L1993"></a>**L1993** `01:16:07` allow all they stand for to fade into

<a id="L1994"></a>**L1994** `01:16:09` obscurity. The Woolin have come and

<a id="L1995"></a>**L1995** `01:16:12` despite Heath Moore's hasty attempt to

<a id="L1996"></a>**L1996** `01:16:14` stem the tide and hold back the

<a id="L1997"></a>**L1997** `01:16:16` invaders, the knights, samurai, and

<a id="L1998"></a>**L1998** `01:16:17` Vikings found themselves left on the

<a id="L1999"></a>**L1999** `01:16:20` back foot against a force they had not

<a id="L2000"></a>**L2000** `01:16:22` foreseen. The war for Heathmore would

<a id="L2001"></a>**L2001** `01:16:24` never be the same. And it now dawned on

<a id="L2002"></a>**L2002** `01:16:27` the dennisens of Heathmore that they

<a id="L2003"></a>**L2003** `01:16:29` were not alone in this world and their

<a id="L2004"></a>**L2004** `01:16:31` neighbors in foreign lands may be just

<a id="L2005"></a>**L2005** `01:16:33` as dangerous as the ones from China.

<a id="L2006"></a>**L2006** `01:16:39` Marching Fire. Man, what a time to be

<a id="L2007"></a>**L2007** `01:16:41` alive. 2018

<a id="L2008"></a>**L2008** `01:16:44` was kind of at like a dry point in its

<a id="L2009"></a>**L2009** `01:16:45` life. Sure, they were still pumping out

<a id="L2010"></a>**L2010** `01:16:46` updates, but nothing was really that

<a id="L2011"></a>**L2011** `01:16:48` big. Marching Fire was a huge update.

<a id="L2012"></a>**L2012** `01:16:51` I'd still say it's Foner's biggest

<a id="L2013"></a>**L2013** `01:16:52` update in its entire life. Basically, a

<a id="L2014"></a>**L2014** `01:16:54` whole rebrand. You have an entire new

<a id="L2015"></a>**L2015** `01:16:57` faction, all four heroes with all their

<a id="L2016"></a>**L2016** `01:16:59` little unique things to it. A lot of

<a id="L2017"></a>**L2017** `01:17:01` characters back then, you can kind of

<a id="L2018"></a>**L2018** `01:17:02` point and be like, "Oh, that's kind of

<a id="L2019"></a>**L2019** `01:17:04` the same as that. That's the same as

<a id="L2020"></a>**L2020** `01:17:05` that." Everyone in the Wuin brought

<a id="L2021"></a>**L2021** `01:17:06` something new to the table that was

<a id="L2022"></a>**L2022** `01:17:08` never seen before in the game. And all

<a id="L2023"></a>**L2023** `01:17:10` of them were unique in their own ways.

<a id="L2024"></a>**L2024** `01:17:12` But not only the Wuin were added, but so

<a id="L2025"></a>**L2025** `01:17:14` many other things like Breach, for

<a id="L2026"></a>**L2026** `01:17:16` example. Froner's game modes at the time

<a id="L2027"></a>**L2027** `01:17:18` were very dull and just boring, right?

<a id="L2028"></a>**L2028** `01:17:20` Things like elimination, skirmish, and

<a id="L2029"></a>**L2029** `01:17:22` tribute were already dead in the water

<a id="L2030"></a>**L2030** `01:17:24` one year into its game's release. But

<a id="L2031"></a>**L2031** `01:17:25` Breach was this huge thing. We had this

<a id="L2032"></a>**L2032** `01:17:28` huge update, new game mode, new

<a id="L2033"></a>**L2033** `01:17:29` mechanics, and huge new maps on a new

<a id="L2034"></a>**L2034** `01:17:32` scale. I think people were really

<a id="L2035"></a>**L2035** `01:17:34` wanting some larger scale fights and I

<a id="L2036"></a>**L2036** `01:17:36` think people were really missing that in

<a id="L2037"></a>**L2037** `01:17:37` the multiplayer segment. So Breach kind

<a id="L2038"></a>**L2038** `01:17:39` of brought that part into For Honor. Now

<a id="L2039"></a>**L2039** `01:17:42` is Breach perfect? Not really. But no

<a id="L2040"></a>**L2040** `01:17:43` game mode is really perfect. But again,

<a id="L2041"></a>**L2041** `01:17:45` you cannot deny the overall positive

<a id="L2042"></a>**L2042** `01:17:48` aspect for Honor's Marching Fire update

<a id="L2043"></a>**L2043** `01:17:50` brought. I mean, it almost quadrupled

<a id="L2044"></a>**L2044** `01:17:52` the player base on PC alone on one day

<a id="L2045"></a>**L2045** `01:17:55` of it being out. Obviously, it trickled

<a id="L2046"></a>**L2046** `01:17:57` down, but that was huge, especially for

<a id="L2047"></a>**L2047** `01:17:59` PC. PC was really struggling with

<a id="L2048"></a>**L2048** `01:18:01` players. It was really hard to find a

<a id="L2049"></a>**L2049** `01:18:03` match. Sometimes you'd go 10, 15, 20

<a id="L2050"></a>**L2050** `01:18:05` minutes. No game found whatsoever. On

<a id="L2051"></a>**L2051** `01:18:08` the days of Marching Fire, I remember

<a id="L2052"></a>**L2052** `01:18:09` queuing up for 5 seconds for Breach when

<a id="L2053"></a>**L2053** `01:18:11` it first came out and boom, instantly.

<a id="L2054"></a>**L2054** `01:18:13` Everyone PC, everyone was so happy to

<a id="L2055"></a>**L2055** `01:18:15` finally get matches as fast as possible.

<a id="L2056"></a>**L2056** `01:18:17` It was a perfect time to be playing the

<a id="L2057"></a>**L2057** `01:18:19` game after a long drought of 10, 15

<a id="L2058"></a>**L2058** `01:18:21` minute Q times where you'd have to

<a id="L2059"></a>**L2059** `01:18:23` reset. Marching Fire is definitely the

<a id="L2060"></a>**L2060** `01:18:26` biggest step for Honor took into keeping

<a id="L2061"></a>**L2061** `01:18:28` itself alive.

<a id="L2062"></a>**L2062** `01:18:35` day one he released. I got to say this,

<a id="L2063"></a>**L2063** `01:18:37` people thought he was broken. Not only

<a id="L2064"></a>**L2064** `01:18:38` did he come with all sorts of crazy

<a id="L2065"></a>**L2065** `01:18:40` [ __ ] I'm talking infinite combo, the

<a id="L2066"></a>**L2066** `01:18:42` triple lights, he introduced something

<a id="L2067"></a>**L2067** `01:18:44` to the game that no one else saw coming,

<a id="L2068"></a>**L2068** `01:18:45` which is the keystandance tech, which is

<a id="L2069"></a>**L2069** `01:18:46` amazing. This is where you do like a

<a id="L2070"></a>**L2070** `01:18:48` light key stance into a heavy key stance

<a id="L2071"></a>**L2071** `01:18:50` and you can still end up in

<a id="L2072"></a>**L2072** `01:18:51` keystandance. He also had a Viper

<a id="L2073"></a>**L2073** `01:18:52` retreat. There was even a flicker tech

<a id="L2074"></a>**L2074** `01:18:54` where he was able to go into

<a id="L2075"></a>**L2075** `01:18:55` keystandance twice. who do an

<a id="L2076"></a>**L2076** `01:18:56` unblockable heavy and land back into

<a id="L2077"></a>**L2077** `01:18:58` keyand where he can do an unblockable

<a id="L2078"></a>**L2078** `01:19:00` heavy or kick you. You really have to

<a id="L2079"></a>**L2079** `01:19:01` have an open mind on how you wanted to

<a id="L2080"></a>**L2080** `01:19:03` pressure somebody because they didn't

<a id="L2081"></a>**L2081** `01:19:04` have openers. But Shalon is definitely

<a id="L2082"></a>**L2082** `01:19:05` been my top tier freaking character. And

<a id="L2083"></a>**L2083** `01:19:08` I had my own Shaolin school teaching

<a id="L2084"></a>**L2084** `01:19:10` people how to play, you know, Shaolin

<a id="L2085"></a>**L2085** `01:19:11` very patiently. This character brought

<a id="L2087"></a>**L2087** `01:19:15` crazy stuff. The best thing I like about

<a id="L2089"></a>**L2089** `01:19:18` teleport across the map, the fact that

<a id="L2094"></a>**L2094** `01:19:26` do this martial art in real life and I

<a id="L2095"></a>**L2095** `01:19:28` was 5 years old going up to 14 15 years

<a id="L2096"></a>**L2096** `01:19:30` old where I had to learn how to balance

<a id="L2097"></a>**L2097** `01:19:31` on a staff. People were so competitive

<a id="L2098"></a>**L2098** `01:19:33` with this character. It's funny. Have

<a id="L2099"></a>**L2099** `01:19:35` you ever realized when you fought a

<a id="L2100"></a>**L2100** `01:19:36` Shaolin they all have the same

<a id="L2101"></a>**L2101** `01:19:38` personality with that taunting and that

<a id="L2102"></a>**L2102** `01:19:39` emote stuff? Yeah, that all came from

<a id="L2103"></a>**L2103** `01:19:41` me. I'm sorry.

<a id="L2104"></a>**L2104** `01:19:44` >> Marching Fire was the biggest update in

<a id="L2105"></a>**L2105** `01:19:46` For Honor history. We got a new faction,

<a id="L2106"></a>**L2106** `01:19:49` four new heroes, two new game modes, two

<a id="L2107"></a>**L2107** `01:19:52` events, three new maps, and visual

<a id="L2108"></a>**L2108** `01:19:54` enhancements. The patch notes would take

<a id="L2109"></a>**L2109** `01:19:56` an hour to read. The update requires 60

<a id="L2110"></a>**L2110** `01:19:59` gigs of space, and the only thing left

<a id="L2111"></a>**L2111** `01:20:01` out to the disappointment of a lot of

<a id="L2112"></a>**L2112** `01:20:03` people was no woolen faction to actually

<a id="L2113"></a>**L2113** `01:20:06` pick as your faction, making the whole

<a id="L2114"></a>**L2114** `01:20:08` faction war seem redundant now that you

<a id="L2115"></a>**L2115** `01:20:11` can play heroes that aren't even

<a id="L2116"></a>**L2116** `01:20:12` involved in it. Tundi, a Vanguard, was a

<a id="L2117"></a>**L2117** `01:20:16` Chinese warrior with a curved blade and

<a id="L2118"></a>**L2118** `01:20:18` balike movement. He had infinite dodge

<a id="L2119"></a>**L2119** `01:20:20` heavy attacks and a quick undable dodge

<a id="L2120"></a>**L2120** `01:20:23` light. He also had a neutral bass

<a id="L2121"></a>**L2121** `01:20:25` similar to what Warlord has, but a

<a id="L2122"></a>**L2122** `01:20:27` pretty basic kit. Besides that, his

<a id="L2123"></a>**L2123** `01:20:28` undable lights could be spammed

<a id="L2124"></a>**L2124** `01:20:30` indefinitely, making him hell to fight

<a id="L2125"></a>**L2125** `01:20:32` for for the first few weeks until people

<a id="L2126"></a>**L2126** `01:20:34` learned how to actually react to that.

<a id="L2127"></a>**L2127** `01:20:36` While most casuals enjoyed him, people

<a id="L2128"></a>**L2128** `01:20:38` became quickly annoyed by the voice

<a id="L2129"></a>**L2129** `01:20:40` actress of the female version, screaming

<a id="L2130"></a>**L2130** `01:20:42` on her voice lines. As for the

<a id="L2131"></a>**L2131** `01:20:43` competitive scene, he became a staple of

<a id="L2132"></a>**L2132** `01:20:46` highlevel duels and remained in the

<a id="L2133"></a>**L2133** `01:20:47` upper half of the tier lists ever since.

<a id="L2134"></a>**L2134** `01:20:50` Nia wasn't received anywhere near as

<a id="L2135"></a>**L2135** `01:20:52` well though. An assassin with two hook

<a id="L2136"></a>**L2136** `01:20:54` swords and a very basic tree chain kit

<a id="L2137"></a>**L2137** `01:20:56` with her main staple being her unique

<a id="L2138"></a>**L2138** `01:20:58` traps. If you try to block, parry, or

<a id="L2139"></a>**L2139** `01:21:00` counter heavies, and she would faint to

<a id="L2140"></a>**L2140** `01:21:02` traps, she'd punish you. Meaning the

<a id="L2141"></a>**L2141** `01:21:04` only way to beat it was to purposefully

<a id="L2142"></a>**L2142** `01:21:07` block in another direction or interrupt

<a id="L2143"></a>**L2143** `01:21:09` her. While it's unique, people found

<a id="L2144"></a>**L2144** `01:21:11` many ways to counter it. and you'd

<a id="L2145"></a>**L2145** `01:21:13` quickly realize that she is one of the

<a id="L2146"></a>**L2146** `01:21:14` worst heroes in the game with the only

<a id="L2147"></a>**L2147** `01:21:16` good tool she has being a fast lifespan.

<a id="L2148"></a>**L2148** `01:21:18` I even made a montage showcasing all her

<a id="L2149"></a>**L2149** `01:21:20` flaws a few years back and pointing at

<a id="L2150"></a>**L2150** `01:21:23` the fact that she has been pretty much

<a id="L2151"></a>**L2151** `01:21:24` left behind in fawn. From the weakest to

<a id="L2152"></a>**L2152** `01:21:26` the best, we had Jangjun or JJ for

<a id="L2153"></a>**L2153** `01:21:29` short. A personification of the

<a id="L2154"></a>**L2154** `01:21:30` historical Chinese general Guanu with

<a id="L2155"></a>**L2155** `01:21:33` his long h and surprisingly agile

<a id="L2156"></a>**L2156** `01:21:35` movement considering how old he is. He

<a id="L2157"></a>**L2157** `01:21:37` had a seafo stance which would

<a id="L2158"></a>**L2158** `01:21:38` regenerate his own stamina and from

<a id="L2159"></a>**L2159** `01:21:40` which he could throw zones. Besides

<a id="L2160"></a>**L2160** `01:21:42` that, he could also dodge attack forever

<a id="L2161"></a>**L2161** `01:21:44` similar to Tandi and had just a very

<a id="L2162"></a>**L2162** `01:21:46` funny personality. I always like he was

<a id="L2163"></a>**L2163** `01:21:49` also considered very powerful too. His

<a id="L2164"></a>**L2164** `01:21:51` safness punishing other heroes was

<a id="L2165"></a>**L2165** `01:21:53` extraordinary. In an era where

<a id="L2166"></a>**L2166** `01:21:55` everything was reactable, his defensive

<a id="L2167"></a>**L2167** `01:21:57` play style for the win baby 400 ms bash

<a id="L2168"></a>**L2168** `01:21:59` faintable and it was fundamentally

<a id="L2169"></a>**L2169** `01:22:01` unreactable. It just provided him with

<a id="L2170"></a>**L2170** `01:22:03` very strong offense. On top of that, we

<a id="L2171"></a>**L2171** `01:22:05` had 40 damage orange UFO flying towards

<a id="L2172"></a>**L2172** `01:22:09` you at the speed of light. Cuzone

<a id="L2173"></a>**L2173** `01:22:11` provided a consistent 18 plus damage

<a id="L2174"></a>**L2174** `01:22:14` punish against almost every character

<a id="L2175"></a>**L2175** `01:22:16` read that he did against other heroes.

<a id="L2176"></a>**L2176** `01:22:18` And on top of that, his speeds were just

<a id="L2177"></a>**L2177** `01:22:21` marvelous, right? Bonkers, boys.

<a id="L2178"></a>**L2178** `01:22:23` Bonkers. The second new game mode

<a id="L2179"></a>**L2179** `01:22:25` released was against bots only. Arcade.

<a id="L2180"></a>**L2180** `01:22:28` You would go on four levels, beating

<a id="L2181"></a>**L2181** `01:22:30` bots with certain attributes and

<a id="L2182"></a>**L2182** `01:22:31` sometimes even with some law. with

<a id="L2183"></a>**L2183** `01:22:33` increasing difficulty making it tough to

<a id="L2184"></a>**L2184** `01:22:35` beat especially the weekly quests on

<a id="L2185"></a>**L2185** `01:22:37` Halloween. Then we got Return of the

<a id="L2186"></a>**L2186** `01:22:39` Otherworld, a throwback event of Feast

<a id="L2187"></a>**L2187** `01:22:41` of the Otherworld.

<a id="L2188"></a>**L2188** `01:22:43` >> It was a buffed game mode variant than

<a id="L2189"></a>**L2189** `01:22:46` the compared to the first one that

<a id="L2190"></a>**L2190** `01:22:48` released. I think there was higher

<a id="L2191"></a>**L2191** `01:22:49` minion bonuses during minion kill land.

<a id="L2192"></a>**L2192** `01:22:52` You would just get lit up with purple

<a id="L2193"></a>**L2193** `01:22:54` fire and you just ran around just

<a id="L2194"></a>**L2194** `01:22:58` absolutely unleashing unholy damage on

<a id="L2195"></a>**L2195** `01:23:00` the whole enemy team. It was kind of

<a id="L2196"></a>**L2196** `01:23:02` wild. If you got hit once, it was like

<a id="L2197"></a>**L2197** `01:23:05` you clear minions, you go kill somebody.

<a id="L2198"></a>**L2198** `01:23:07` That's how it went,

<a id="L2199"></a>**L2199** `01:23:09` but uh this was a pretty fun game event.

<a id="L2200"></a>**L2200** `01:23:11` Not much more to talk about this one cuz

<a id="L2201"></a>**L2201** `01:23:13` it was pretty standard. You know,

<a id="L2202"></a>**L2202** `01:23:15` >> during Christmas then we received FA's

<a id="L2212"></a>**L2212** `01:23:36` like you still have a chance if you lose

<a id="L2216"></a>**L2216** `01:23:45` lane. But uh it's kind of difficult if

<a id="L2235"></a>**L2235** `01:24:26` >> Season 8 went down for most as the

<a id="L2236"></a>**L2236** `01:24:27` greatest season in For history. And

<a id="L2237"></a>**L2237** `01:24:29` thanks to a free week, the player base

<a id="L2238"></a>**L2238** `01:24:31` skyrocketed. They revealed that the next

<a id="L2239"></a>**L2239** `01:24:33` season would again have four new heroes,

<a id="L2240"></a>**L2240** `01:24:35` one for each faction and one for each

<a id="L2241"></a>**L2241** `01:24:36` season. And Ubisoft made clear that they

<a id="L2242"></a>**L2242** `01:24:39` were far from over with this silly sword

<a id="L2243"></a>**L2243** `01:24:41` cape.

<a id="L2244"></a>**L2244** `01:24:45` The gear system was a relic of Fore

<a id="L2245"></a>**L2245** `01:24:47` Honor's past. You had your three basic

<a id="L2246"></a>**L2246** `01:24:49` gears of revenge, attack, and defense.

<a id="L2247"></a>**L2247** `01:24:52` This led to a lot of different variable

<a id="L2248"></a>**L2248** `01:24:54` builds with characters. So, if you

<a id="L2249"></a>**L2249** `01:24:56` wanted a very tanky warlord with things

<a id="L2250"></a>**L2250** `01:24:58` like tough as nails and just any way to

<a id="L2251"></a>**L2251** `01:25:00` make your character tanky, you would

<a id="L2252"></a>**L2252** `01:25:02` spec into the defense stat, but that

<a id="L2253"></a>**L2253** `01:25:03` would leave your attack lacking compared

<a id="L2254"></a>**L2254** `01:25:05` to other people. And obviously, your

<a id="L2255"></a>**L2255** `01:25:06` revenge gain would be less. Or if you

<a id="L2256"></a>**L2256** `01:25:09` want to go the other side, you can go a

<a id="L2257"></a>**L2257** `01:25:10` damage boosting build with berserker,

<a id="L2258"></a>**L2258** `01:25:12` fear itself, and fury to just basically

<a id="L2259"></a>**L2259** `01:25:15` delete people with two to three heavies.

<a id="L2260"></a>**L2260** `01:25:16` I think this was a great way to do it,

<a id="L2261"></a>**L2261** `01:25:18` but obviously the problem that came

<a id="L2262"></a>**L2262** `01:25:20` through it was the fact that the revenge

<a id="L2263"></a>**L2263** `01:25:22` build was just the go-to meta for the

<a id="L2264"></a>**L2264** `01:25:24` time because why would you do anything

<a id="L2265"></a>**L2265** `01:25:25` else other than want more revenge? Now,

<a id="L2266"></a>**L2266** `01:25:28` moving to the perk system, I think it

<a id="L2267"></a>**L2267** `01:25:30` was kind of a necessary evil that they

<a id="L2268"></a>**L2268** `01:25:32` had to do. As much as I like the gear

<a id="L2269"></a>**L2269** `01:25:33` system, it had to happen because there

<a id="L2270"></a>**L2270** `01:25:35` was no way to balance it without pissing

<a id="L2271"></a>**L2271** `01:25:37` somebody off. Where I had problems with

<a id="L2272"></a>**L2272** `01:25:39` the perk system is that obvious perks

<a id="L2273"></a>**L2273** `01:25:41` are better than others. You have the

<a id="L2274"></a>**L2274** `01:25:43` vanguard tree, which is normally just

<a id="L2275"></a>**L2275** `01:25:46` okay. You have things like endurance,

<a id="L2276"></a>**L2276** `01:25:47` which is great, head hunter, which is

<a id="L2277"></a>**L2277** `01:25:48` fantastic. But compared all of that to

<a id="L2278"></a>**L2278** `01:25:51` the heavy perk tree, everything pales in

<a id="L2279"></a>**L2279** `01:25:53` comparison to eventual barrier, last

<a id="L2280"></a>**L2280** `01:25:55` standed fashion. These three perks rule

<a id="L2281"></a>**L2281** `01:25:57` the game. No diff questions asked. I

<a id="L2282"></a>**L2282** `01:26:00` think what they did wrong is that

<a id="L2283"></a>**L2283** `01:26:02` there's too many perks in the perk tree

<a id="L2284"></a>**L2284** `01:26:04` that are very useless or do the exact

<a id="L2285"></a>**L2285** `01:26:06` same thing. The support tree is my

<a id="L2286"></a>**L2286** `01:26:08` biggest gripe. You have three to four

<a id="L2287"></a>**L2287** `01:26:11` perks in the game that just increase

<a id="L2288"></a>**L2288** `01:26:13` your speed. You have feline agility,

<a id="L2289"></a>**L2289** `01:26:14` supersonic, radiant rebound. All of

<a id="L2298"></a>**L2298** `01:26:36` The faction war had never been as tense

<a id="L2463"></a>**L2463** `01:32:51` With the Vikings and samurai forced to

<a id="L2468"></a>**L2468** `01:33:00` allies hot on their trails, the samurai

<a id="L2469"></a>**L2469** `01:33:02` hadn't suffered losses this extreme in

<a id="L2470"></a>**L2470** `01:33:04` years. And now they were at risk of

<a id="L2471"></a>**L2471** `01:33:06` having everything fall in on them. In an

<a id="L2472"></a>**L2472** `01:33:07` act of defiance and perhaps even

<a id="L2473"></a>**L2473** `01:33:09` madness, the samurai lured the Viking

<a id="L2474"></a>**L2474** `01:33:12` forces to a village deep in the jungle

<a id="L2475"></a>**L2475** `01:33:13` of their home known as the Canopy. The

<a id="L2476"></a>**L2476** `01:33:16` Vikings, certain that the samurai would

<a id="L2477"></a>**L2477** `01:33:18` be cornered here, gave chase without

<a id="L2478"></a>**L2478** `01:33:19` thought or hesitation. Instead of weak

<a id="L2479"></a>**L2479** `01:33:21` and frightened samurai, they discovered

<a id="L2480"></a>**L2480** `01:33:23` a secret weapon of the meer that had

<a id="L2481"></a>**L2481** `01:33:25` they had not anticipated. The Hitokiri.

<a id="L2482"></a>**L2482** `01:33:29` Once they were simply executioners,

<a id="L2483"></a>**L2483** `01:33:31` dealers of judgment to criminals and

<a id="L2484"></a>**L2484** `01:33:33` evildoers, but being surrounded by death

<a id="L2485"></a>**L2485** `01:33:35` and sin all their lives corrupted their

<a id="L2486"></a>**L2486** `01:33:36` view of the world, building in these

<a id="L2487"></a>**L2487** `01:33:38` warriors a terrifying philosophy that

<a id="L2488"></a>**L2488** `01:33:40` life is painful and death is its

<a id="L2489"></a>**L2489** `01:33:43` release. salvation. And to the poor and

<a id="L2490"></a>**L2490** `01:33:46` suffering souls in this world, they will

<a id="L2491"></a>**L2491** `01:33:48` offer this release as a mercy to protect

<a id="L2492"></a>**L2492** `01:33:51` the Meer from their mercy. The emperor

<a id="L2493"></a>**L2493** `01:33:53` put tight sanctions and leashes upon the

<a id="L2494"></a>**L2494** `01:33:55` Heakiri.

<a id="L2495"></a>**L2495** `01:33:57` Until now. Now, as long as their brand

<a id="L2496"></a>**L2496** `01:34:00` of mercy was given only to the enemies

<a id="L2497"></a>**L2497** `01:34:01` of the Meer, they were now free to act

<a id="L2498"></a>**L2498** `01:34:04` as they saw fit. And the Vikings would

<a id="L2499"></a>**L2499** `01:34:06` feel their compassion firsthand. Blood

<a id="L2500"></a>**L2500** `01:34:09` flowed through the forest as the axes of

<a id="L2501"></a>**L2501** `01:34:11` the Hitakahiri severed heads, spines,

<a id="L2502"></a>**L2502** `01:34:13` and limbs like twigs. Terrified and

<a id="L2503"></a>**L2503** `01:34:16` broken twice, the Vikings were again

<a id="L2504"></a>**L2504** `01:34:17` forced to abandon their invasion and

<a id="L2505"></a>**L2505** `01:34:19` escape to Valenheim with their tails

<a id="L2514"></a>**L2514** `01:34:37` and the Meer, perhaps it was time they

<a id="L2517"></a>**L2517** `01:34:43` handle it after the way they'd been

<a id="L2518"></a>**L2518** `01:34:45` dealt with by Sakura.

<a id="L2790"></a>**L2790** `01:45:37` the ground and starts levitating and

<a id="L2791"></a>**L2791** `01:45:39` makes a big red indicator where he's

<a id="L2792"></a>**L2792** `01:45:41` going to land. And once he lands and

<a id="L2793"></a>**L2793** `01:45:43` drops that hammer, it's a big onehit

<a id="L2794"></a>**L2794** `01:45:46` kill lightning strike.

<a id="L2795"></a>**L2795** `01:45:49` This [ __ ] was very fun.

<a id="L2796"></a>**L2796** `01:45:54` >> While the three factions fought their

<a id="L2797"></a>**L2797** `01:45:56` desperate wars for power, releasing

<a id="L2798"></a>**L2798** `01:45:58` their harbingers upon one another, the

<a id="L2799"></a>**L2799** `01:46:00` Wuin were consolidating their own

<a id="L2800"></a>**L2800** `01:46:01` resources and forces. At Chang Pass,

<a id="L2801"></a>**L2801** `01:46:04` Emperor Gao had his finest alchemist and

<a id="L2802"></a>**L2802** `01:46:07` adviser, Sunda, along with his

<a id="L2803"></a>**L2803** `01:46:08` specialist warriors, the Zan Hu, working

<a id="L2804"></a>**L2804** `01:46:11` tirelessly on weapons and projects to

<a id="L2805"></a>**L2805** `01:46:13` give them an edge in the coming wars for

<a id="L2806"></a>**L2806** `01:46:14` Heathmore. However, unexpectedly, a

<a id="L2807"></a>**L2807** `01:46:17` massive explosion in Sunda's tower put

<a id="L2808"></a>**L2808** `01:46:19` the entire fortress on the defensive and

<a id="L2809"></a>**L2809** `01:46:21` completely unawares. With the Woolen

<a id="L2810"></a>**L2810** `01:46:23` struggling to figure out what had

<a id="L2811"></a>**L2811** `01:46:24` happened and what had been the cause,

<a id="L2812"></a>**L2812** `01:46:26` another force appeared. An unknown

<a id="L2813"></a>**L2813** `01:46:28` legion of knights, bearing colors and

<a id="L2814"></a>**L2814** `01:46:30` banners unknown to the Wuen. This

<a id="L2815"></a>**L2815** `01:46:32` legion, the remnants of the Blackstone

<a id="L2816"></a>**L2816** `01:46:34` Legion, appeared and attacked in force.

<a id="L2817"></a>**L2817** `01:46:36` The Wuin mounted a desperate evacuation

<a id="L2818"></a>**L2818** `01:46:38` attempt to get their emperor to safety,

<a id="L2819"></a>**L2819** `01:46:40` but to no avail. They were caught,

<a id="L2820"></a>**L2820** `01:46:43` captured, and held by the Blackstones in

<a id="L2821"></a>**L2821** `01:46:45` their mysterious new leader. Emperor

<a id="L2822"></a>**L2822** `01:46:47` Galet was exiled back to China in shame

<a id="L2823"></a>**L2823** `01:46:50` and defeat. And Sunda, too brilliant and

<a id="L2824"></a>**L2824** `01:46:52` cunning to leave alone, was recruited by

<a id="L2825"></a>**L2825** `01:46:54` this mysterious leader for purposes then

<a id="L2826"></a>**L2826** `01:46:56` unknown. As for the Woolen themselves,

<a id="L2827"></a>**L2827** `01:46:58` all were forced into servitude of this

<a id="L2828"></a>**L2828** `01:47:00` new power that was rising, and only

<a id="L2829"></a>**L2829** `01:47:02` those that were not at Chang Pass during

<a id="L2830"></a>**L2830** `01:47:04` the siege were left to their own

<a id="L2831"></a>**L2831** `01:47:05` devices. But every Wuan would curse the

<a id="L2832"></a>**L2832** `01:47:08` name that they felt had betrayed them.

<a id="L2833"></a>**L2833** `01:47:12` Sunda

<a id="L2834"></a>**L2834** `01:47:16` >> Year 3 season 4 saw the release of a new

<a id="L2835"></a>**L2835** `01:47:18` William character Zanu. Zanu is

<a id="L2836"></a>**L2836** `01:47:21` aesthetically very similar to Ken, a

<a id="L2837"></a>**L2837** `01:47:23` hero with a long sword and an elegant

<a id="L2838"></a>**L2838** `01:47:25` personality. He was the first and last

<a id="L2839"></a>**L2839** `01:47:27` hero to have unblockable finish and

<a id="L2840"></a>**L2840** `01:47:28` light attacks. He also had two dodge

<a id="L2841"></a>**L2841** `01:47:30` attacks, both unreactable and coming

<a id="L2842"></a>**L2842** `01:47:32` from opposite sides.

<a id="L2843"></a>**L2843** `01:47:34` >> Zonu on release really wasn't that good.

<a id="L2867"></a>**L2867** `01:48:23` either you're either too bad or just the
